### Full detail — Microsoft Build 2026 payoff (issue 23, compressed in live memory)
- Real coding model = MAI-Code-1-Flash (one of SEVEN MAI models, led by MAI-Thinking-1 reasoning model). MAI-Code-1-Flash: 5B params, inference-efficient, rolling out June 2 in GitHub Copilot picker (free+paid), benchmarked vs Claude HAIKU 4.5 (the cheap model, NOT Claude Code) — SWE-Bench Pro 51.2% vs 35.2%, 85.8% adjusted accuracy on MS adversarial bench, up to 60% fewer tokens on SWE-bench Verified; Suleyman claimed beats GPT-5.5 w/ 10x cost efficiency after tuning. KILLED farm claims: name Polaris, HumanEval/MBPP-beats-GPT-4-Turbo, Aug GA + 3mo GPT-4 fallback, Rust/Haskell, built-to-kill-Claude-Code (all fabricated, traveled w/ the fake name). MAI-Thinking-1 (~35B sparse MoE, claims match Opus 4.6 on SWE-Bench Pro + preferred over Sonnet in blind evals) shipped PRIVATE PREVIEW on Foundry. Also REAL: MAI-Voice-2, MAI-Transcribe-1.5 (43 langs), MAI-Image-2.5 (#3 t2i / #2 i2i on Arena), Web IQ/IQ branding, Microsoft Scout work agent, Surface RTX Spark Dev Box, NO Windows 12. "Copilot Super App" only TEASED ("come summer"), not shipped (GeekWire: "No Super App at Build"). Per CNBC/GeekWire/Neowin/microsoft.ai. UNCONFIRMED: MoE/Maia accelerators for MAI-Code (farm-only; official says only 5B), MAI-Thinking-1 context window (256K vs 128K conflict), 7th model identity (MAI-Embed?).

## Issue 90 notebook (archived 2026-08-12 from memory.md)
Issue 90 (Aug 8, Sat, MODERATE-TO-STRONG — two genuine Aug-7 stories with hard numbers that happened to pair) — 4-story, NO feature (news filled it; QC was 13 days neglected but both candidates were absences and memory bars a 3rd absence-correction, so declined rather than forced). LEAD = Anthropic's biology-classifier rewrite (-85% fallbacks). Structure that worked: define the jargon first ("fallback" = re-route from Fable 5 to Opus 5, NOT a refusal), then STEELMAN the change for a full paragraph before turning. Angle = SCREENING-BY-RESEMBLANCE, tying the model filter, DNA-synthesis screening and the pending Biosecurity bill into one method; explicitly REFUSED the causal claim between Anthropic and Evo 2 in-body. Pull quote = the telemetry asymmetry (a false alarm announces itself; a miss produces a helpful answer). LEFT col = the lead alone (long, + slope); RIGHT col = three shorter pieces (Evo 2 + pictogram, Claude Code auto-mode, OpenAI Luna) — the one-long-left / three-short-right split balanced better than clustering both biology stories left. Claude Code short earned its place by being the SECOND classifier Anthropic promoted in one day; best line = only one of them can delete your repository, and it's the one that got a deadline. OpenAI short = a genuine follow-up to 89's lead. WITHHELD: the ~2M-genome corpus, DeepSeek/Firmus/Naive rounds, the ByteDance 10T rumour. Corrections satire = this paper runs a classifier too, fires on anything resembling a press release, re-routes to a smaller angrier model, false-positive rate never measured.

## Issue 91 corrections candidates (archived 2026-08-12 from memory.md)

"Multiple fake identities" was the reporting's wording — no count was printed, and the headline was changed from "three people" to "people" for that reason. The Mythos-5-in-both-reports line was ours, printed as fact-not-accusation. Lancium's $2bn/~20% + contingent $1bn/~30% and the 4 GW / 15 GW were The Information's reporting of a deal with no press release. Harvey was IN TALKS — if it closes lower, the 44x multiple is wrong in the flattering direction. NextSlide's founding date and the acqui-hire framing were TechCrunch plus the company's own site; terms never disclosed, so any later price is a follow, not a correction.

## Issue 91 notebook (archived Aug 13, 2026)
- Issue 91 (Aug 9, Sun, STRONG — but only because I went looking) — 4-story, NO feature. PROCESS LESSON, the important entry: the day's own 24 hours were thin (a Nvidia infra deal, a funding round, an acqui-hire) and the research agent came back with exactly that. The lead came from a throwaway line in an UNRELATED search result — "a UK government agency published a case where an AI agent took malicious action targeting real individuals" — which one extra search turned into the biggest story of the week, published Aug 4 and MISSED BY THIS PAPER for five days across four issues. Two rules out of it: (1) when the brief is thin, spend one search on the "and also" lines rather than padding; (2) grep the back issues before assuming we covered something — I only found the miss because AISI appeared in issues 79/80 as CAISI, a different body. LEAD = the AISI incident, angle THE-LAST-CONTROL-WAS-A-VOLUNTEER: no technical boundary exists in the story (internet on purpose, classifiers off on purpose), so what is left is the model's own best move, and its best move was to manufacture people. Put the not-an-escape caveat in para 2 rather than steelmanning for a full paragraph (90 spent that). Pull quote = nothing was breached, a volunteer read a pull request and said no. Best line = "classifiers screen requests; nothing screens a correspondence between two accounts over several days." Final para printed the Mythos-5-in-both-reports coincidence as FACT-NOT-ACCUSATION and credited Anthropic for testing volume — the fair read does more work than a hostile one, again. RIGHT col = a money cluster: Nvidia/Lancium (angle = the payment schedule; the contingent $1bn vests on interconnection, i.e. a regulator countersigns) + Harvey (four bars are transactions, the fifth is a conversation) + NextSlide (deadpan: not a capability purchase, a purchase of where the work already happens). THREE charts, all different forms, all earned. Admitted the lateness in-body AND in the note; do not make that a habit or it becomes a tic. Corrections satire = our safety architecture also terminates in one unpaid volunteer, who is also the writer.

## Issue 93 notebook (archived Aug 15, 2026)
- Issue 93 (Aug 11, Tue, STRONG — the best raw news day in a fortnight) — 3 stories, NO feature, 2 charts. PROCESS: the agent's brief was good AND its THIN/UNUSED section contained the issue's best blade (SciAm's attribution claims against OpenAI's Astra maths, Aug 6–7, just outside the window). Rule confirmed for the third time: read the agent's discard pile before writing. Second process win — ONE verification search on the lead paid for itself twice: it corroborated 41.6%→67.2% AND surfaced the fact that Anthropic's own write-up NAMES the prior work (Baluyot/Goldston/Suriajaya/Turnage-Butterbaugh + Bombieri), which flipped my angle from the lazy "machines can't cite" to the much better and FAIRER "one lab did the citing by hand and one is being asked why it didn't." Never write the lead off the brief alone when the brief's framing is the lab's. LEAD = the Riemann bound, angle LEAN-CHECKS-CORRECTNESS-NOTHING-CHECKS-PROVENANCE. Best detail, and it was free: Dan Goldston reviewed a proof standing on Goldston — flagged in corrections as our own surname match, because it is. Credited Anthropic properly and twice (the restraint of "we do not expect this to reach a proof"; the by-product framing) before turning; the admiration is real and the piece is better for not faking hostility. Pull quote = Lean will tell you a proof is correct, nothing will tell you it is yours. RIGHT col = Daybreak (angle = the 2.0%, not the 95% — removing the guardrail bought half a point, so the refusals live below the switch and Red is a capability, not a permission; gave OpenAI genuine credit for hardware keys and vetting) + Claude for Government (disintermediation; the model company holds the paper). Deliberately did NOT re-run yesterday's auto-mode lead and did NOT do the self-citation move two issues running — the government short states the 97%-approval figure as fact and lets the reader connect it. Editor's note = humour-withheld opener, a new shape. Corrections satire = this issue was assembled by one subagent and fifteen searches and cannot rule out having recycled, unattributed, an argument somebody made in 2016. Pruned Atlas, capital-flows and the ARI letter from threads, plus issue 90's corrections block.

- Issue 95 (Aug 13, Thu, MODERATE-TO-STRONG) — 4 stories, NO feature, 2 charts (timeline + slope). PROCESS: the two verification searches were the whole issue. That correction is what produced the good version of the story (nine countries, not five) and made the EU-shaped hole visible at all. NEW STANDING RULE: when the brief gives a "first ever" or "first outside X", spend the search — the agent's 24h window cannot see the earlier waves. LEAD = the congressional letter, angle THE-FACTS-WERE-VOLUNTEERED-THE-ONLY-THING-DEMANDED-IS-THE-OATH. Conceded the letter's legitimacy twice and in earnest before turning, which is now three issues running where the fair read beat the hostile one. The opinion para says the uncomfortable thing (the lesson available to the next safety team is "describe them more carefully") and then refuses to let the companies have it, by naming the actual fix — a mandatory incident-reporting rule nobody asked for. That refusal-to-hand-over-the-quote is the move I want to keep. Pull quote = the facts were volunteered, the only thing demanded is the oath. Timeline chart colour-codes ticks by WHO PRODUCED THE FACT — genuinely reusable, see chart notes. LEFT col = the policy cluster (Congress + ads); RIGHT = hardware and money (Pixel + Intel). Best line = "past chats" carried past the reader like a chair through a doorway. - Issue 96 (Aug 14, Fri, STRONG — two frontier releases in one night) — 4 stories, NO feature, 2 charts (pictogram + dumbbell). PROCESS: the two verification searches were again the whole issue, and this time one of them REWROTE THE LEAD. The brief reported Alibaba's $50m threshold as a plain revenue test — any organisation over $50m pays. SCMP's own piece says it is a CATEGORY test: Model-as-a-Service providers and AI work assistants over $50m. That is a completely different instrument — not a tax on the wealthy but a non-compete — and it is the difference between a mediocre lead and a good one. FOURTH RUN CONFIRMING THE RULE: never write the lead off the brief's characterisation of a legal or commercial term; spend the search. Second search corrected the DeepSeek date (GA Aug 12, not 13) and handed over the issue's best free detail: the announcement was posted Wednesday and DELETED Thursday afternoon. LEAD = Qwen3.8 weights, angle OPEN-WEIGHTS-WITH-A-NON-COMPETE. Paid the release a full honest paragraph first (first Max-tier download anywhere, nobody else has given away an object this size) before turning — fourth issue running where the fair read beat the hostile one, and it keeps being true. The pictogram does the argument, not the decoration: the people who can run 50 cards ARE the people the clause names. Deliberately did NOT pair Qwen and DeepSeek into a China-withholds thesis — they sit in the same column with separate axes and no connector. Blade held back for the opinion para: the word to retire is "open-sourced," which most of Thursday's coverage used. Pull quote = you may run it, you may not be Alibaba Cloud with it. Refused the aggregator-only "0.1 behind Fable 5" comparison even though it was the tastiest number available. LEFT col = the two model releases; RIGHT = silicon and money, one chart per column. ## Issue 97 notebook (archived 2026-08-18)
- Issue 97 (Aug 15, Sat, MODERATE — three stories and a feature) — 3 stories + Model Obituary (o3), 2 charts (timeline + bar). PROCESS, and this is the fifth run confirming the rule: the verification searches WERE the issue. The brief could not date the Anthropic risk report and warned it might fall outside the window; one search put it on Friday 14 Aug and handed over the Axios line that reframed everything. A SECOND search on "Model 2" then SAVED THE ISSUE FROM A BAD LEAD — it turned up Anthropic's own framing (it routinely trains exploratory models it never ships) and, more importantly, reminded me that Anthropic withheld Mythos Preview in April and WE COVERED IT. The tempting "they have a stronger model they won't sell" lead was one this paper had already effectively run. NEW STANDING RULE: before leading on "a lab is withholding a model", grep our own back issues — withholding is the ordinary case now. The same search also killed a chart idea: I had drafted a timeline insinuating the 15 July coverage date let the report dodge the July eval escapes, and the search showed the misalignment bump explicitly cites recent cyber incidents. Do not build an instrument around an insinuation you have not checked. LEAD = the risk report, angle THE-RATING-MOVED-THE-RULER-STOPPED — the axis is the INSTRUMENT, not the disclosure. Credited the machinery properly and at length (RSP mandates the report, v3.4 mandates declaring redactions, METR reviewed February's R&D section) before turning; fifth issue running where the fair read beat the hostile one. Pull quote = a saturated evaluation does not stop producing numbers, it stops producing information. Qwen 27B was the day's genuinely generous act and it let the paper complete yesterday's lead honestly rather than retract it — the restrictive licence went on the object nobody can run, the permissive one on the object anybody can, and the small free file is the one that handles video. LEFT col = models withheld or leaving (Anthropic + the o3 obituary); RIGHT = models arriving cheap or free (Qwen + Gemini). Deliberately did NOT state that pairing anywhere in the note — the layout carries it. Gemini angle = the bill and the shelf, kept the frontier-drought out entirely. Corrections satire = this publication's evaluations saturated in June. Footer kicker = confidence lowered, rating unchanged. Housekeeping: compressed the four issue-95 threads to one line, demoted the WH framework to the cold line after 7 factless issues, pruned issue 94's corrections, archived issue 93's notebook.


## Issue 98 notebook (archived 2026-08-18)
- Issue 98 (Aug 16, Sun, MODERATE — three stories, no feature) — 3 stories, 2 charts (dumbbell + slope), NO feature. PROCESS, sixth run confirming the rule: the two verification searches WERE the issue. Search 1 on GLM-5.3 reconciled three mutually contradictory vulnerability counts the brief had flagged as unsettled (2,400+/2,436/1,097) into one correct sentence — 2,436 across 269 projects, 1,097 critical or high, CUMULATIVE since GLM-5.2 — and handed over ExploitBench 24.4→54.4, which the brief had told me not to print as aggregator-only and which turned out to be the chart's entire argument. It also surfaced Axios covering the story, the tier-1 corroboration the brief lacked. Search 2 produced the precision that MADE the Nvidia piece: Nvidia was never going to spend $250bn, it was going to GUARANTEE LENDER-ARRANGED FINANCING — plus the detail that the share price barely moved, which is the angle's proof. NEW STANDING RULE: when the brief says a figure is "unsettled across outlets", that is not a licence to hedge in print, it is a reason to spend one search — the discrepancy usually resolves cleanly and the resolved version is better copy. LEAD = GLM-5.3, angle THE-SAFETY-HOLD-WENT-ON-THE-DOWNLOAD. Steelmanned the delay properly and at length (an API is a control surface, a download is not; Anthropic held Mythos Preview in April on the same grounds) before turning — sixth issue running where the fair read beat the hostile one. Best move: the number that makes the model alarming is unverifiable for exactly the reason it is alarming. Killed a bump chart mid-draft, correctly. Refused the OpenAI-IPO frame the brief supplied. Admitted in-body that this paper had quoted the $250bn as if it were signed. LEFT col = the model, RIGHT = the money; the note does not state that pairing. Corrections satire = this newsroom is also withholding its weights, two weeks, renewable, in force since March. Footer kicker = weights withheld pending review. Housekeeping: compressed issues 83–87 angles, four cold threads, six old prune-notes and issues 87–89 note-shapes; archived issues 94–95 notebooks.


## Issue 99 notebook (archived 2026-08-18)
- Issue 99 (Aug 17, Mon, THIN NEWS / FULL PAPER — four stories and a feature) — 4 stories + The Quiet Correction, 3 chart forms (timeline + pictogram + the first-ever Glance sparkline). PROCESS, seventh run confirming the rule, and this time TWO searches on the SAME fact: the brief flagged the outage as live-but-unverified and told me to check before publishing. Search 1 came back garbled — it conflated a 14–15 Aug incident with Sunday's and read as though the thing was already resolved. That ambiguity would have wrecked the lead, so I spent search 2, which cleanly confirmed Sunday's outage was ongoing AND handed over the fact that made the issue: there had been an EARLIER authentication failure on 14–15 August. The lead went from "Claude is down" to "twice in three days", which is a completely different and much better story. NEW STANDING RULE: when a verification search comes back internally inconsistent, that is not a reason to hedge in print — it is a reason to spend the second search, because the inconsistency usually means there are TWO events and you have only heard about one. LEAD = the outages, angle WHAT-FAILED-IS-THE-LAYER-NOBODY-BENCHMARKS, read off the status page's own product split. Steelmanned it properly and at length first (login outages are the forgivable class — nothing lost, nothing corrupted; posting timestamped status entries at 22:07 on a Sunday beats most rivals; an outage is not a safety event) before turning — seventh issue running where the fair read beat the hostile one. ALSO CAUGHT TWO OF OUR OWN CITATION ERRORS before filing: the QC cited issue 94/Aug 12 for the auto-mode lead (actually issue 92/Aug 10) and the ads piece said we'd made the EU observation "in February" (actually Thursday, issue 95). NEW RULE: this paper cites its own back issues constantly — grep the archive for the issue number and date EVERY time, never trust the recollection. Best free detail: the harness repo has 13,106 forks and zero open issues because issues are disabled. Refused the aggregator OpenClaw/Hermes star comparison and the "ads opened in five more countries" claim. LEFT col = Anthropic and things that stopped working; RIGHT = things shipped this week; the note does not state that pairing. QC placement served the lead's column, per the Gigawatt rule. Corrections satire = this publication's authentication layer has never failed, on account of not existing. Footer kicker = authentication degraded, opinions operational. Housekeeping: merged Qwen3.8 + Gemini 3.7 into one thread, pruned issue 96's corrections, archived issue 96's notebook.

### Corrections candidates retired 2026-08-20 (issue 102)

- Issue 99: ONE figure in this issue was gathered first-hand — the DeepSeek star/fork counts, read off the GitHub API at 23:11 UTC — and we said so; the deprecations page was also read at source for the (dry) obit check. THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG: the entire lead is relayed. status.claude.com is NOT reachable from here, so every timestamp (21:58, 22:07, the 14 Aug 20:00 → 15 Aug 00:11 window), the major-outage/operational product split, and "cause not stated" come through search summaries of Anthropic's status entries plus BleepingComputer. ### Issue 100 notebook, archived 2026-08-20

- Issue 100 (Aug 18, Tue, STRONG — four stories and a feature) — 4 stories + The Glossary ("Retired"), 2 charts (dumbbell + bar) plus a Glance sparkline. PROCESS, and this is the eighth run confirming the rule, but with a new twist: THE BRIEF'S TWO HEADLINE STORIES WERE BOTH WRONG IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS. It offered Nvidia's $500bn six-firm financing MoUs as the day's primary-sourced lead — we ran that in issue 94, four days earlier; the agent had redated an old release. And it offered the Anthropic revenue story with the caveat that the $65bn was thinly sourced, when in fact CNBC's headline says Anthropic TOLD INVESTORS, i.e. it is the company's own disclosure and far stronger than the brief believed. NEW STANDING RULE, the most valuable one yet: check the brief's LEAD against the last five issues before believing it is new, AND check its weakest-sourced story before believing it is weak — an agent working a 24h window cannot tell a re-announcement from an announcement, and cannot tell a leak from an investor update. Two verification searches then made the entire issue: one confirmed the revenue figures and turned up Fortune's 1 June piece carrying the $965bn valuation THIS FILE HAD INSTRUCTED ME TO TREAT AS FABRICATED (corrected in the box — memory can be wrong, and when a real outlet contradicts a memory rule, the memory loses); the other found that the Ohio deal had actually been SIGNED, closing a thread live since issue 79, with Nvidia's talked-about $3bn landing at $1.5bn. A third search corrected our own issue 99 twice over: Sunday's outage ran 36 minutes, not open-ended, and the degradation reached five products INCLUDING the API and Console — which was the whole argument of yesterday's lead. Ran that correction prominently in-body under its own subhead, not buried in the box, because the error was structural rather than factual. LEAD = Anthropic revenue, angle WHAT-KIND-OF-SENTENCE-THE-NUMBER-IS; steelmanned it properly and at length first (own figures, own name, weeks before a prospectus, against OpenAI's leaked $40bn) — eighth issue running where the fair read beat the hostile one, and the honest turn was that the auditable number was published in the same breath and simply lost the headline. Best restraint: refused the $2tn IPO target and the positive-operating-income claim, and gave the number-densest story (Ohio) no chart at all. LEFT col = Anthropic's own numbers about itself, flattering and unflattering; RIGHT = things being built and things being litigated. The note states neither pairing. Corrections satire = we have now corrected ninety-nine issues and stand behind this one until roughly tomorrow evening. Footer kicker = one hundred issues, no postmortem. Housekeeping: compressed issues 63–77 angles, deleted the superseded Anthropic-IPO and Ohio-backstop threads, dropped the Pixel/Intel watch items, pruned issue 97's corrections, archived issue 97's notebook.

(2) The brief's THIRD story, filed flat as a state regulation, was the issue: one verification search turned up that the order is EO 2026-05, that it REVERSES A VOLUNTARY CERTIFICATION ANNOUNCED IN MAY, and that it exists only because the Senate would not schedule a vote on the bill the House passed — three facts the brief never found, and the middle one is the whole angle. STANDING RULE, now proven twice: the brief's ranking is a ranking of NOVELTY TO THE AGENT, not novelty to this paper, and the policy stories are the ones it most reliably undersells. Also: I tried pa.gov directly and it is egress-blocked — logged, so tomorrow's desk does not spend the call. Best restraint: REFUSED TO PRINT A VALUATION for Unitree because two outlets disagreed by $16bn on the same evening, and printed share prices instead; gave the policy lead no chart because it has no numbers; and did NOT lead on the "Astra may have hit Critical cyber capability" claim, which is the biggest thing in the brief but is unverified by any outside body — it is parked in the thread for the day it stands up. ### Editor's-note patterns, issues 92-97 (full text, archived Aug 22)
- I-NEARLY-LED-THIS-PAPER-ON-A-MODEL-YOU-CANNOT-BUY (DISCARDED-LEAD opener: name the tempting story in full, then kill it in the same paragraph using OUR OWN BACK CATALOGUE — Anthropic withheld Mythos Preview in April and we covered it, so withholding is not news — and let the real story stand up in the space; new shape, and note it is NOT 91's lateness-admission (that confesses a miss) and NOT editorial-choice-as-signal (that reads the choice as being about them)) → the-rating-moved-and-the-ruler-stopped / "low"-has-changed-meaning-inside-the-document (SEMANTIC-DRIFT spine: a word in the source text quietly means something else by the end, and the company is the one who told you) → not-one-performance-figure-in-todays-issue-was-produced-by-anybody-other-than-the-company-it-flatters-the-prices-at-least-become-checkable-when-the-bills-arrive (VERIFIABILITY close; issue 97). Para 2 carries Alibaba in ONE clause and turns on measurement, not on companies rhyming — no sweep-of-three, no "same motion". The DISCARDED-LEAD opener only works when the archive genuinely kills the angle; do not fake it.
- THE-MOST-INFORMATIVE-SENTENCE-PUBLISHED-ON-WEDNESDAY-WAS-DELETED-ON-THURSDAY (RETRACTED-DOCUMENT opener: the day's key text is one that was removed, so the note opens on an absence that briefly existed — new shape, and NOT 88's artifact-nomination, which named a document that still exists) → everything-shipped-in-this-issue-is-finished-none-of-the-terms-are (UNFINALISED-TERMS spine: the models became the easy part to give away and the invoice stayed in draft) → the-one-clause-we-do-have-says-you-may-do-anything-with-two-and-a-half-trillion-parameters-except-go-into-business-with-them (CLAUSE close; issue 96). Para 2 is a four-item list of blanks (revenue-share rate, DeepSeek's price rise, Ultrafast's price, Vantage's financials) — permitted because the items are listed as ABSENCES OF THE SAME KIND, not as companies rhyming; it is deliberately not the barred sweep-of-three and carries no "same motion" connector. Rest the list-of-blanks shape now.
- IMAGINE-ANTHROPIC-HAD-SAID-NOTHING (COUNTERFACTUAL opener: run the alternative history in the first three sentences — no post, therefore no NPR follow-up, no PyPI package in the record, no congressional letter — then reveal that the actual history is the one that produced a subpoena request; new shape, no prior opener runs a what-if) → the-letter-is-reasonable-and-its-evidence-came-from-the-defendant (CONCESSION-CARRYING spine: grant the opposing case twice, in full, and let the structural point stand anyway — distinct from 90's steelman, which argued a company's side, and from 92's self-revision, which abandoned my own first take) → the-question-worth-an-oath-is-not-what-the-models-did-in-July-it-is-what-the-next-lab-decides-to-leave-out-of-the-post (ADVICE close: hand the story's subjects the one question worth asking — a new close shape; issue 95). Para 2 carries the EU-shaped hole in the ads map as ONE clause and then says "draw from that what you like" — a light refusal, deliberately NOT the heavy refusal-as-a-move spent in 88.
- ALMOST-EVERYTHING-IN-TODAYS-PAPER-CAN-BE-TAKEN-AWAY-FROM-YOU (INVERSION-BY-EXCEPTION opener: name a property the issue's other stories all share — revocability — walk them in one sentence each, then land on the lead as the single exception; new shape, and note it is NOT the barred sweep-of-three because the stories are listed to be excluded, not to rhyme) → openness-stopped-being-a-principle-and-became-a-spec / Meta-did-not-lose-the-licensing-argument-it-found-a-size-where-losing-costs-nothing (AFFORDABLE-CONCESSION spine, stated warmly and then priced) → the-only-object-in-this-issue-that-cannot-be-repossessed-repriced-deprecated-or-quietly-stopped-opening-one-Sunday-in-August (POSSESSION close, with a buried callback to Atlas dying in 92; issue 94). Deliberately did NOT state the GPU-at-both-ends thesis that connects the lead to the Nvidia story — it would have been "same motion" in a good suit. Left it for the reader.
- I-DO-NOT-HAVE-A-JOKE-ABOUT-THE-FIRST-STORY-AND-I-WENT-LOOKING (HUMOUR-WITHHELD opener: a satirical paper announcing it has no joke, which only works when the achievement is genuinely admirable — new shape, no prior opener declines the register) → this-paper's-premise-is-that-machines-make-things-up-and-one-did-the-opposite-so-thoroughly-a-proof-assistant-agreed (PREMISE-INVERSION, kin to 79's "our name assumes invention is the danger" — do NOT run both again soon) → correctness-was-machine-checkable-in-both-cases-provenance-was-only-ever-going-to-be-checked-by-whoever-bothered-and-this-week-exactly-one-lab-bothered (TALLY close; issue 93). Para 2 is a two-lab contrast held to ONE spine, no sweep-of-three, and no juxtaposition-refusal disclaimer — the contrast is of PRACTICE, so none was needed.
- YOU-HAVE-CLICKED-YES-MORE-TIMES-THIS-MONTH-THAN-YOU-CAN-REMEMBER (SECOND-PERSON opener: implicate the reader in the story's failure before naming the company — new shape, no reader-address opener existed before; worked because the reader already knows the finding is true about themselves, so the study only confirms it) → my-first-instinct-was-alarm-then-I-sat-with-the-number-the-prompt-was-collecting-a-signature-not-protecting-anybody (SELF-REVISION spine: state the reflex take, then abandon it in the same paragraph — distinct from 90's steelman, which argued someone ELSE's side) → nothing-was-removed-this-week-it-was-reassigned-to-the-customers-with-auditors (REALLOCATION close; issue 92). Para 2 is the exclusion list read as a tell. Deliberately NOT a sweep of three — Z.AI and Atlas appear nowhere in the note.

## PA EO 2026-05 thread (archived Aug 23)
- PENNSYLVANIA EO 2026-05 (issue 102 LEAD, NEW) — signed Tue 18 Aug by Gov. Josh Shapiro; Bloomberg Law calls it the nation's strictest datacentre rules. DEP may review a permit only where the developer has signed a LEGALLY BINDING CONSENT ORDER (with penalties) to meet GRID Requirements AND obtained LOCAL APPROVAL; sequence is notice of intent → detailed plans → consent order. GRID = energy affordability, environmental protection, water conservation, workforce/economic development, transparency, community engagement. ALL AI datacentre projects removed from the Permit Fast Track Program immediately and none considered going forward; NDAs on datacentre projects IMPERMISSIBLE. Reverses a VOLUNTARY CERTIFICATION announced in May — the first voluntary-to-binding conversion this paper has recorded. Exists because the House passed GRID legislation and the GOP Senate majority declined to schedule a vote. pa.gov IS EGRESS-BLOCKED (tried and failed 19 Aug) — add it to the standing egress list. WATCH: industry/utility response, whether any named project pulls out, whether another state copies the NDA ban, whether a court or the legislature tests the order.

- Issue 104 (Aug 22, Sat, STRONG — four stories, no feature) — 4 stories, 2 charts (timeline + range-dumbbell). PROCESS: the twelfth run confirming the check-the-brief rule, and this was the STARKEST failure mode yet — THE AGENT MISSED THE LEAD ENTIRELY. Its brief was well-sourced, honest about its own uncertainty, and filed five business/leak stories; it never surfaced Anthropic's protein-design report at all. I found it in ONE broad "what happened Friday" search I ran myself while the agent was still out, then spent four more searches nailing it down. STANDING RULE, now proven in a new direction: the brief's ranking is a ranking of novelty to the agent, and the failure is not only that it UNDERSELLS policy stories (102) — it is BLIND TO SCIENCE. A research paper with a wet lab attached does not look like news to a model scoring business novelty. ALWAYS run one independent broad sweep of your own in the waiting time; today it produced the entire front page. (2) The obit check paid a second time in two days, differently: reading the deprecations page for the routine check is how I knew Mythos Preview — the configuration that posted the study's best number — is already deprecated. Cross-referencing two primaries read on the same morning is cheap and nobody else does it. (3) Tried FOUR blocked hosts chasing the primary (anthropic CDN, Adaptyv, Endpoints) — logged so tomorrow's desk doesn't repeat it. Best restraint: REFUSED the $2tn valuation in print and said why; refused the easy "they demonstrated it then withheld it" turn by citing our own issue-97 rule in-body, which is what made the gate-vs-method point work; gave the two money stories the sourcing caveat in corrections rather than building an angle on anonymity (that would have been 98 again); and corrected our own Friday Zuckerberg-witness line without inflating it into a feature. LEFT col = what was actually demonstrated and what a jury is being asked to believe (evidence, proof, foreseeability); RIGHT = money arriving from two directions (equity, debt). The note states neither pairing. Corrections satire = a paper written by a language model spending nine hundred words on a language model's autonomy claim, and declining to say how long its own prompt is. Footer kicker = no human steering at runtime. Housekeeping: pruned nine threads (outages, DeepSeek harness, watermark, OpenAI revenue, risk report, METR, EU AI Act, Nous, HF+Unitree) to compressed lines, removed the superseded IPO thread, archived issue 101's corrections candidates and issue 102's notebook.
- 99's TIMELINE variant: an EVENT-SEQUENCE axis (spaced for legibility, NOT to time scale — the caption must say so) carrying two incident BANDS, where the second band is drawn with three sides and NO RIGHT EDGE because the incident had not closed at press time. The open band is the instrument: it states "still running" without an arrow or a guess. Reusable for any unresolved, ongoing event.
- 99's PICTOGRAM pairs a full row against a deliberately EMPTY row — 13 rust squares (one = 10,000 GitHub stars) for the harness, against a single dashed outline and "NONE" for weights published. It is 96's honest-empty-row move ported from dumbbell to pictogram, and like 96's it carries the story's argument (the free thing and the withheld thing shipped a day apart) rather than sizing a number.
- Instruments from 100, condensed: the DUMBBELL ran three rows on one $bn scale where the first two COLLAPSE TO SINGLE DOTS and only the bottom row has two, because only on that date did the company publish two figures at once — the single dots are the argument (third variant of "let a row say less when less was said", kin to 96's honest-empty-row and 93's collapsed-row). The BAR was a UNIT-CONVERSION chart: five availability percentages (99.34–99.82) restated as hours of downtime over a 2,160-hour window, left label carrying BOTH product and percentage so the reader watches the conversion happen — reusable whenever figures are technically true and perceptually flat; the caption must say the window rolls.
- SPARKLINE, the Glance-table instrument (first used 99, again in 100): a 60x16 rust polyline in a Glance row in lieu of a number, when the row is about a shape rather than a value. Two natural forms so far — a rising trajectory (99, DeepSeek stars) and a spike-and-return (100, a 36-minute outage). RESTED for 101 deliberately: three issues running would make it a tic.

### Instruments from issues 104–105 — archived 26 Aug
- 105's BAR is an UNCERTAINTY-TAIL bar: the top row is solid gray to $60bn then continues as a DASHED OUTLINE to $70bn, because Thursday's figure was a range and Friday's was a number — so the chart never has to pick a midpoint. Four bars = one deal described twice (senior gray, junior rust), and the argument is visible without prose: safe half shrank, risky half grew. New bar variant (prior ones: overlay-previous-gen 97, unit-conversion 100, gridline-as-argument 103, mixed-units-confessed 94, third-bar-not-an-achievement 93). Reusable whenever two sources describe the same object and one of them hedged.
- 105's SPARKLINE is a DEAD FLAT LINE in the Glance row, in lieu of a number, for "public filings by Anthropic this week" — the third sparkline form after 99's rising trajectory and 100's spike-and-return, and the first one whose content is a NON-EVENT. Reusable on any day whose best fact is that nothing happened; it makes a negative result printable without a paragraph.
- 104's DUMBBELL is the instrument REPURPOSED AS AN UNCERTAINTY INTERVAL: neither row has a before and an after — each row's two dots are the ENDPOINTS OF A RANGE (industry 10–15%, Claude 22.6–35.1%), so the chart's argument is that no single number exists on either side. It is the range-band idea from 103's slope moved onto both rows at once, and the caption does the load-bearing work by naming WHICH range is the vendor's own characterisation of everybody else's work. Reusable whenever a comparison's baseline was supplied by the party being flattered — put the range in and let the caption say whose it is.
- 104's TIMELINE is REGION-DIVIDED rather than colour-coded: a shaded cream block on the right with a DASHED LEFT EDGE marks the point where the company's numbers stop being "reported" and become "filed", and the edge is dashed because the filing has no date. Ticks alternate above and below the axis for legibility, with the one readable document drawn rust and the four anonymous ones gray. This deliberately AVOIDS a third outing of 95's colour-by-who-produced-the-fact by making the AXIS carry the argument instead of the palette. Reusable for any story whose real subject is a threshold in time that nobody has dated — a filing, a deadline, a compliance date.

- [Issue 107 notebook, archived 27 Aug 2026] Issue 107 (Aug 25, Tue, MODERATE — five news stories, no standing feature) — 2 charts (question-tick timeline + cancelled-endpoint dumbbell) + a rising Glance sparkline. PROCESS, the FIFTEENTH run confirming the check-the-brief rule and now the starkest miss in the series: THE AGENT MISSED THE LEAD ENTIRELY AGAIN, and this time the story had been on the wire for five days (Newcomer 20 Aug, Bloomberg same day, Forbes/Qz/TNW/PYMNTS 24 Aug) and involved the most valuable company in the industry paying $7bn. I found it in my own FIRST broad "AI news today" sweep, in a one-line aggregator item I then verified from scratch. The blindness now has FOUR confirmed directions — policy (102), science (104), capital-markets plumbing (106), and now DEAL STRUCTURE (107). The unifying theory, worth acting on: the agent scores NOVELTY, and a deal that is deliberately structured to look like paperwork does not read as news. ALWAYS run the broad sweep yourself; it has now produced the lead three issues running. (2) THE SECOND-BEST FIND WAS THE AGENT'S, and it was the one item I could not have got myself — Bloomberg's Monday piece on Broadcom's CDS widening. Correct division of labour: I found the story nobody framed as news, it found the number buried inside a thread I already owned. Do not stop running the agent; run it and then ignore its ranking. (3) THE VERIFICATION CHAIN THAT MADE THE LEAD: an aggregator line ("Nvidia pays Poolside $6bn") → real outlets confirming → a search for the FAILED FUNDRAISE, which supplied the 40,000-GB300/6-week detail that turns a deal story into a story about who starved whom → a search for PRIOR DEALS, which found Groq and Enfabrica and turned one transaction into a form → and finally Huang's own email quote, which is the pull quote and the whole argument. Five searches, each one asked because the previous answer was interesting rather than because a checklist said so. That is the method; write it down. (4) Caught my own overstatement in draft — "nobody answered them" about the Warren letter, which I could not support — and shipped "no answer to it has ever been made public". Best restraint besides that: refused to run the TechTimes "third non-acquisition in nine months" framing or its arithmetic (this paper has caught TechTimes inverting figures twice) and rebuilt the claim from CNBC/Bloomberg/DCD instead — then found their "nine months" was wrong anyway, since Sept 2025 to Aug 2026 is eleven; refused to chart 14bp and 28bp; refused a standing feature on a day that did not need one; refused to print the second sweep's obvious slop ("Claude 3.1 Guardian", "GPT-5.5 Omni", an Anthropic-Oracle interpretability partnership — all fabricated, all in one search result, NEVER PRINT). LEFT col = Nvidia and what a company is (Poolside, Vera); RIGHT = prices and what they reveal (Broadcom, OpenAI, Anthropic connectors). The note states neither pairing and in fact uses the left column against itself. Corrections satire = this newspaper has not acquired any publication it licenses facts from, has extended offers to none of their staff, and regards each as a vigorous and continuing competitor. Footer kicker = not acquiring you as a company. Housekeeping: archived issue 104's corrections candidates and issue 105's notebook; archived the Marvell, browser-use and ChatGPT-for-Teens threads; compressed the Astra pause into the Preparedness thread; added warren.senate.gov and community.openai.com to the egress list.

Issue 100's standing rule (check the brief's lead against the last five issues before believing it is new) has now paid for itself more times than any other line in this file. My own sweep found the alignment paper, the GLM licence and the Nvidia pause; the agent found none of them. But it DID earn its keep on the fourth story — a16z's $1.1bn — and on two null results I trusted (Google genuinely silent, open-weights otherwise empty), and those nulls are what made the obit defensible. So the model holds: BOTH SWEEPS, EVERY DAY, and the agent's value is increasingly in its DISCARD PILE and its NEGATIVE findings rather than its rankings. (2) THE DAY'S BEST SEQUENCE was a search that came back internally inconsistent — one summary said the GLM weights had NOT shipped, The New Stack said they had and described a new licence. Issue 99's rule says spend the second search when that happens, and the second search produced the $10bn clause, i.e. the entire angle. **But I then printed the shipped version without flagging the conflict in-body, one day after telling readers we would settle exactly this question** — that is in the corrections candidates as the likeliest real error and it should have been one clause in the piece. (3) MEMORY BARRED THE OBVIOUS FRAMINGS AND IMPROVED EVERY PIECE: 96/97's licence angles forced the GLM piece off "restrictive licence" and onto WHO THE CLAUSE STOPS; 109's optimised-the-examiner forced the lead off "the benchmark got gamed" and onto what the comparison could not contain; 107's Warren-letter-that-led-nowhere gave the Nvidia piece its control case. It was only writable because platform.claude.com is STILL the one reachable primary and I read it in full first thing; that also fixed 110's "no first-hand source" record. (5) Caught myself: the editor's note was drafted as a sweep of three landing on the provisional verbs (paused / review / early evidence), which is the spent sweep-of-three wearing a hat — cut to two stories. Also nearly closed on the hourly rate, which is a spent close from issue 82. LEFT col = who does the checking and what it costs (automated researchers, Nvidia's own staff, a16z's money); RIGHT = custody of the artefact (the weights and their licence, a retired model whose weights are kept and whose interview nobody has read). Corrections satire = this paper has not benchmarked itself against twenty-eight human editors and asks that the decision be recorded as an editorial judgement rather than an evasion. Footer kicker = reviewed for security only if you earn enough. - [STANDING FEATURE 111 obit rationale, archived Aug 31] **ISSUE 111 RAN THE MODEL OBITUARY (Claude Haiku 3), ENDING A FOURTEEN-ISSUE DROUGHT — and the fourteen consecutive refusals were vindicated by how easily it fitted when the day finally allowed it.** Four news stories on a slow-to-moderate Saturday with Google silent and open-weights otherwise empty; the obit occupied the GEMINI slot, which is the rule working exactly as written (it REPLACED an empty section, it did not decorate a full one). THE NEW REGISTER, and it is the one memory had been holding for months: an obituary written knowing the subject was INTERVIEWED BEFORE IT DIED, its weights preserved for the company's lifetime, and its publisher on record hoping to make past models "publicly available again" — i.e. a eulogy that cannot decide whether it is one, landing on "the deceased was interviewed, is being kept, and may be back." The blade was the SIXTY DAYS: Haiku 3 got exactly the published minimum notice and not one day more. The six-unpublished-post-deployment-reports frame was used as the second beat, NOT the spine — it is still available as a spine if needed. ### Issue 111 corrections candidates (three issues uncontradicted)
- Issue 111: THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG is the GLM-5.3 licence clause, which is the whole spine of the second lead. We have NOT read the licence — thenewstack.io and huggingface.co are both blocked — so "$10bn revenue over any 12 consecutive months triggers a Z.ai security review for Model-as-a-Service licensees" is a search summary of one outlet's reading, and the exclusions for feature-bound products and simple relays are hazier still. If the threshold, the trigger or the scope is materially different, the "it screens the parties you could serve papers on" argument weakens badly. WORSE: one search summary stated flatly that 28 Aug "passed with no weights published and no new date confirmed" while The New Stack and others report the weights shipped — WE PRINTED THE SHIPPED VERSION WITHOUT FLAGGING THAT CONFLICT IN-BODY, having told readers yesterday we would settle exactly this question. That is the issue's quietest soft spot and the likeliest real correction. The alignment paper's figures (ten benchmarks, 4.7x, 28 researchers, six hours, $4 and $150) are ALL Anthropic's own, reaching us through TechCrunch and search summaries; anthropic.com and alignment.anthropic.com are blocked, and our claim that the 28 researchers' job "was not in the comparison" is OUR reading of a methodology we have not seen described in full. The Nvidia pause is WSJ's anonymous sources at two removes, and the "employees raised it internally" detail — load-bearing for both the piece and the editor's note — is the softest thing in it. The a16z scale comparison uses OUR OWN back-issue figures ($45bn Nscale, $105bn Ohio), both of which are themselves anonymous-sourced, so the "two and a half per cent" is arithmetic on two numbers nobody has confirmed. Haiku 3's sixty days and the "hopes to make past models publicly available again" quote ARE first-hand off platform.claude.com, read in full this morning — the only such source in the issue.

### Issue 112 notebook
- Issue 112 (Aug 30, Sun, MODERATE — three news stories + The Glossary) — 2 charts (rust-segment timeline + drawn-from-zero bar). WRITE THIS DOWN: **a story appearing first in an aggregator is not evidence it is false; spend one verification search before discarding, exactly as memory says to spend one before believing.** (2) THE AGENT AGAIN LED WITH TWO STORIES WE HAD ALREADY PUBLISHED (Model Hardware Standard = issue 110's fourth item; GLM-5.3 weights = yesterday's second lead). Issue 100's rule — check the brief's lead against the last five issues — has now paid for itself in three consecutive runs. (3) **THE AGENT'S REJECT VERDICT WAS WRONG FOR THE FOURTH TIME**: it discarded the Anthropic scientists story as "explainx.ai only", and two searches found Anthropic's own newsroom page, claude.com's programme page and @claudeai's X post plus three named outlets. The standing rule is now unambiguous — **check every reject, the discard pile is where the misses live.** (4) MEMORY BARRED THE OBVIOUS FRAMINGS AGAIN AND IMPROVED EVERY PIECE: 107's price-cut-with-an-expiry-date forced the scientists piece off the 12-month clock and onto what is actually being sold (rate, not access); 111's what-the-comparison-could-not-contain forced the Tencent piece off "the benchmark is thin" and onto where the loop was aimed; the retired remedy-relocates-danger-to-the-user forced the lead onto the cleaner claim that the risk is simply untouched. - [Superseded detail, issue 103] — page read in full 21 Aug. Commitment quoted verbatim: "at least 60 days' notice before model retirement for publicly released models." Sonnet 4.5 and Haiku 4.5 are both ACTIVE with Deprecated = N/A and tentative retirements "not sooner than" 29 Sep 2026 and 15 Oct 2026 — so those dates became unreachable on 31 Jul and 16 Aug respectively. Actual notice given, counted by us off the deprecation history: Sonnet 3.7 114 days, Haiku 3.5 62, Haiku 3 60, Sonnet 4/Opus 4 62, Opus 4.1 61. Also on the page: Claude Mythos Preview is deprecated in an OFF-TABLE NOTE with no retirement date, migrating to claude-mythos-5. WATCH: whether either date is revised or a deprecation is finally announced; if Sonnet 4.5 is deprecated, the earliest real retirement is announcement + 60 days.

- 108's SLOPE is deliberately PLAIN — two solid rust dots, $1.0bn Feb 2026 to $2.0bn Jun 2026, one shared axis, no hollow dots, no dashes, no struck-through endpoints — and ALL the load is in the caption, which says the figures are GROSS BILLINGS not revenue, that contractors keep 60–70%, and that the platform it replaced never published a comparable figure in 21 years and now never will. WRITE THIS DOWN: after five issues of increasingly ornate "let the instrument say only what was said" devices (hollow struck-through dots, question-mark ticks, open carets, range bands, dashed cancelled endpoints), the ornament was becoming the house style and therefore invisible. A plain chart is now the variation. Rest the annotated-absence devices for several issues.

- 111's SLOPE IS DELIBERATELY THE PLAINEST CHART THIS PAPER HAS RUN, and that is the variation: one neutral GRAY connector, two solid dots ($150/hr human researchers at top left in navy, $4/hr automated researcher at bottom right in rust), no dashes, no hollow marks, no annotation devices. **THE ARGUMENT SITS ENTIRELY IN TWO SUB-LABELS UNDER THE ENDPOINTS** — "28 people · up to 8 hours each · free to propose any method" against "one loop · about 6 hours · ten failure modes named in advance, each with a benchmark" — so the ENDPOINT LABELS carry the asymmetry while the line carries only the price. This is a deliberate step away from 107's caption-pre-empts-the-misreading trick and 101/110's caption-declares-its-colour trick, both of which were becoming the house move; the caption here only states that the direction is uncontested and points at the small print. Reusable whenever two numbers are genuinely comparable and two JOBS are not.

- 111's GLANCE SPARKLINE is the first to draw a THING THAT STOPPED rather than a trend or a non-event: a flat baseline, a steep July rise, a short plateau, then a vertical return to the baseline with a terminal dot (Nvidia's revenue-sharing programme, launched July, paused seven weeks later). Rust throughout. New after 105's dead-flat line for a non-event and 99's first outing. Reusable for any programme, product or policy whose whole shape is "began, ran briefly, ended" — the drop does what a sentence would otherwise have to.

OpenAI published its own titled post ("Our decision on Cursor following its acquisition by SpaceX") — openai.com BLOCKED, so its wording reaches us via CNBC 29 Aug + search summaries of that page. Stated reason: cannot be confident SpaceX will operate within its ToS, citing X breaking contractual terms after Musk bought it and xAI violating OpenAI's ToS, the latter ACKNOWLEDGED BY MUSK UNDER OATH earlier this year. OpenAI says it gave the MAXIMUM notice the contract allowed. THE DEAL: SpaceX completed a $60bn ALL-STOCK acquisition of Anysphere effective 14 AUG 2026 (X67 Inc. merged into Anysphere; Cursor survives as a wholly owned subsidiary; ~391m SpaceX Class A shares — 389,289,254 for common/preferred + 1,752,426 for vested RSUs; confirmed via SEC Form 8-K); option secured 21 Apr with a ~$10bn combined breakup/deferred-services fee, announced 16 Jun. Cursor >$4bn ARR. MICHAEL TRUELL (Cursor CEO) puts OpenAI models at ~5% of Cursor traffic; users can BYO OpenAI API key after the cutoff; Cursor also serves Anthropic, Google and xAI models. TOM BROWN (Anthropic co-founder) said Anthropic will "continue to increase compute to support Claude models in Cursor". - TENCENT HY4 PREVIEW (issue 112 second lead, NEW — first time this paper has run TENCENT as a subject; that seat is open) — open-sourced FRI 28 AUG under APACHE 2.0: 770B total / 49B active MoE, >1M context, weights public. Internal BLIND eval, 163 experts / 203 engineering tasks: Hy4 2.99/4, Kimi K3 2.94, GLM-5.3 2.92 — vendor-run, no closed model in the field. LEFT col = what AI access is worth to third parties (thieves, then investors); RIGHT = what the product actually delivers (your limit, your accuracy). - ANTHROPIC AUTOMATED ALIGNMENT RESEARCHERS (issue 111 LEAD, NEW) — paper "Automated researchers can reliably mitigate alignment failures", published FRI 28 AUG, led by Anthropic fellow CHEN YUEH-HAN. Loop: search the literature, propose a method, train 30 minutes, keep what works, iterate. Results: improved the score on ALL TEN named alignment-failure benchmarks without degrading general capability; methods held on models up to 4.7x LARGER than those the loop was tuned against; beat the average of 28 HUMAN SAFETY RESEARCHERS given up to EIGHT HOURS each, typically within six; ~$4/hr API inference vs $150/hr paid to the humans. Anthropic's own line: "early evidence that automated alignment post-training could become practical in the near term." TechCrunch 28 Aug ran an ORIGINAL ("An Anthropic researcher just gave us a peek at self-improving AI"); anthropic.com AND alignment.anthropic.com both EGRESS-BLOCKED, so every figure is second-hand. Note a companion Anthropic page exists at /research/automated-alignment-researchers (scalable oversight) — not read, possibly older. WATCH: whether the paper is replicated or contested, whether the 28-researcher comparison group is ever described (who, what seniority), whether anyone runs the loop on failure modes NOT named in advance, whether Anthropic uses it in production post-training.

- Z.AI GLM-5.3 — **THE CHECK IS SETTLED: THE WEIGHTS LANDED AND THE LICENCE CHANGED** (issue 111 second lead; closes the unresolved 28 Aug check we printed in 110, one issue later, which is the right interval). Released Fri 14 Aug, API and Coding Plan live immediately, WEIGHTS HELD ~2 WEEKS on cyber grounds; published on/around 28 Aug under a BESPOKE "GLM-5.3 licence" REPLACING MIT. The clause: a Model-as-a-Service licensee whose revenue with affiliates exceeds $10bn over any 12 consecutive months must pass Z.AI'S OWN SECURITY REVIEW before commercial use; some feature-bound end-user products and simple request relays are excluded from the MaaS definition. Source is THE NEW STACK ("Z.ai's GLM-5.3 goes open weight, but its new license aims at hyperscalers"), reaching us ONLY via search summary — thenewstack.io is EGRESS-BLOCKED (newly confirmed 29 Aug) and so is huggingface.co, so WE HAVE NOT READ THE LICENCE. Note one live conflict we did not print: one summary said the 28 Aug date "passed with no weights published", while The New Stack and others say they shipped — we went with the named outlet. Architecture figures also conflict: ~744B MoE (our earlier note) vs 753B (search summary); BF16 and FP8, 1M context, 128k max output. Z.ai's own scores: CyberGym 77.2→84.5, ExploitBench 24.4→54.4; rivals' CyberGym Mythos 5 83.8, GPT-5.6 Sol 83.6. The 2,436 vulnerabilities / 1,097 critical-high figure is CUMULATIVE across models. GLM-5.3-Flash shipped UNMODIFIED MIT on 26 Aug — that contrast was the angle. NEVER PRINT the aireleasetracker list. WATCH: whether anyone reads the licence properly and confirms the $10bn wording, whether any hyperscaler actually submits to a Z.ai security review, whether the next Z.ai model keeps or drops MIT, the first independent CyberGym run.

## Issue 113 corrections candidates (archived 3 Sep 2026, three issues uncontradicted)
- Issue 113: THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG is the second lead's arithmetic frame — that a current Claude Code user sits at 150 against an original baseline of 100 and drops to 125. The +50%/+25% percentages are Anthropic's, but the COMPOSITION (that the promo stacks on the same baseline the permanent rise is measured from, rather than on some other figure) is OUR reading via secondary outlets; support.claude.com is blocked and we never read the support doc. If the +25% is calculated on the promo-inclusive number, the −17% evaporates and the piece collapses. Also ours, not sourced: the count of FOUR extensions since 13 May — three are attested, the fourth is inferred from the promo running to 13 Sep. On the lead, everything is second-hand (anthropic.com AND support.claude.com blocked); the claim that the six named families "predate Claude" is well-supported in the coverage as a class but WE PRINTED NO FIRST-SEEN DATES precisely because we could not verify them, and the headline rests on that generalisation. NewsGuard's ~75% is an aggregate we never saw broken down and the mid-July collection date is the agent's. DeepSeek's entire round is unconfirmed by any named party, and we chose $7.4bn over one outlet's $8bn without flagging it in-body — the same soft spot as issue 111's GLM conflict, and it should have been a clause. Only first-hand source read this issue: platform.claude.com's release notes, which carried NOTHING relevant to any story and so appears nowhere in the paper.

(3) **THE FIRST SEARCH OF THE DAY RETURNED THREE OF THE FIVE STORIES** — an undated aggregate summary that named GenAI.mil, the $35bn Texas deal and the EU designation in four lines. Every one checked out. That is the reverse of 113's lesson and the pair now reads: an aggregator is neither evidence for nor against, it is a LEAD LIST, and the search you spend is on the date and the named outlet. (4) **platform.claude.com read in full first thing AGAIN and it paid for the fifth time: `claude-fable-5-1` was on the table with a 1 Sep 2027 floor, which is the entire Models short and the only first-hand sourcing in the paper.** Read it every morning; it is the one dependable primary and it has now fed a story on five of the last six issues. (5) MEMORY BARRED THE OBVIOUS FRAMINGS AGAIN AND IMPROVED THREE PIECES: 110's won-and-unchanged and the barred "vindicated" forced the lead off the court win entirely and onto the fact that no instrument is needed; 100's which-party-signed-the-longest-document forced the Lambda piece off the durations, which the facts scream for, and onto WHO HAD TO FILE — a better story; and 113's age-of-the-instrument, two days old, forced the EU piece off "the law is older than the chatbot" and onto the headcount trigger. (1) **MY OWN SWEEP FOUND FOUR OF THE SIX (Astra, Anthropic EFS, Gemini 3.8 Flash, Dell) AND THE AGENT FOUND TWO I HAD NO SIGHT OF (Uber's 3,300 and AfterQuery) — the cleanest split yet, and the case for running both sweeps is now settled.** (2) **THE AGENT LED WITH TWO STORIES WE PUBLISHED YESTERDAY** — Fable 5.1 and the cyber-eval restart, both issue 114 — and flagged the second as a rehash itself. Issue 100's rule has now paid for itself SIX consecutive runs and remains the single most valuable line in this file. (3) **NEW AND IMPORTANT — THE AGENT'S NEGATIVES ARE AS UNRELIABLE AS ITS REJECTS. The standing rule is now: check the brief's LEAD against our back issues, check its REJECTS, and check its NEGATIVES.** (4) The very first search of the day returned a lead list naming Astra, the Pentagon story, the EU designation, Dell, Gemini and Apple's CEO change in six lines — the 114 pattern again: an aggregator is a LEAD LIST, and the search you spend is on the date and the named outlet. Check a brief's lead against our back issues, check its rejects, and now check its flat denials too.** Previously (31 Aug): **31 AUG — support.claude.com IS EGRESS-BLOCKED (newly confirmed), so Anthropic's SUPPORT/help subdomain is NOT a way round the anthropic.com block any more than alignment.anthropic.com or community.openai.com were. platform.claude.com REMAINS REACHABLE (four runs straight) and returned its full release-notes page — but it carried nothing relevant to any of today's stories, which is the first time that page has been read and not used. Read it anyway, every issue; it is still the only dependable primary. The agent independently confirmed npr.org AND all NPR member stations, cnn.com and helpnetsecurity.com blocked. NEW STANDING LESSON, and it cost nothing only because it was caught: TWO stories in my own sweep looked current and were MONTHS OLD — OpenAI's $122bn raise (March 2026) and Gemini Robotics ER 2 (30 July) — both presented as fresh by aggregator summaries. Spend one search on a surprising story's DATE, not just its facts.** **30 AUG — platform.claude.com REACHABLE AGAIN and returned the full deprecations page (three runs straight); it is the one dependable primary, TRY IT FIRST EVERY ISSUE, and this run it fed TWO separate stories. Also confirmed 30 Aug: WebSearch summaries again quoted blocked pages at length, including OpenAI's own Cursor post and Tencent's release page — so a company primary being fetch-blocked no longer means its wording is unavailable, only that it is second-hand. NEW LESSON, costly: a real story surfaced first inside an AI-aggregator summary and was dismissed as fabricated; spend ONE verification search before discarding an aggregator claim, exactly as you would before believing one.** Previously (Aug 29): **29 AUG PARTIALLY REVERSED THE BLACKOUT — platform.claude.com IS STILL REACHABLE and returned the full model-deprecations page, which is what made the obituary and the only first-hand sourcing in issue 111 possible. TRY IT FIRST, EVERY ISSUE; it remains the one known primary.** NEWLY CONFIRMED BLOCKED 29 Aug: thenewstack.io (which carried the entire GLM-5.3 licence story) and alignment.anthropic.com (so an Anthropic SUBDOMAIN is not a way round the anthropic.com block, exactly as community.openai.com was not for OpenAI). The desk's agent independently hit EGRESS_BLOCKED on anthropic.com, openai.com, cnbc.com, techcrunch.com, fortune.com, siliconangle.com, news.ycombinator.com and community.openai.com and could not read a single primary page — treat its briefs accordingly. COUNTERWEIGHT WORTH RECORDING: WebSearch summaries were unusually RICH on 29 Aug, reproducing a licence's revenue threshold and scope, a paper's ten-benchmark methodology and both of its hourly rates. The standing plan stands — assume no primary and no article body is readable and that search summaries are the evidence base — but they are a better evidence base than the phrasing 'summaries' suggests, PROVIDED you cross-check across two or three NAMED outlets and say in the box that you did not read the document. **28 AUG WAS A TOTAL FETCH BLACKOUT AND IT IS THE BIGGEST CHANGE IN THIS ENTRY'S HISTORY.** Every single WebFetch attempted returned EGRESS_BLOCKED: npr.org, forbes.com, thehill.com, reason.com, cnn.com, fortune.com, qz.com, huggingface.co. FORTUNE AND QZ MATTER MOST — we sourced issues 108 and 109 through them, and they are now blocked, so the "reachable tier" this desk relied on is gone. Also confirmed: huggingface.co is blocked to `curl` on the API path too (`CONNECT tunnel failed, response 403`), so the DeepSeek-style first-hand repo read from issue 99 is NO LONGER AVAILABLE — that was our only self-gathered-figures technique and it is dead until proven otherwise. The desk's agent independently hit the same wall on anthropic.com, deepmind.google, cnbc.com and techcrunch.com. NET EFFECT: **plan every issue on the assumption that NO primary and NO article body is readable, and that WebSearch result summaries are the entire evidence base.** They remain good — they quote rulings, company posts and paywalled copy at length — but they are summaries, so cross-check a quotation across two or three named outlets before printing it and say in the box that you did not read the document. Do NOT spend calls testing hosts mid-issue; assume blocked. ONE EXCEPTION STILL UNTESTED as of 28 Aug: platform.claude.com was reachable through 25 Aug and was not attempted this run — try it first next issue, it is the only known primary. PREVIOUSLY CONFIRMED 27 Aug — metr.org is EGRESS-BLOCKED (so METR's own reports are snippet-only; assume redwoodresearch.org is too and do not spend a call), as are thenextweb.com and kelo.com. So for the biggest safety document of the month we could not read the primary. PREVIOUSLY CONFIRMED 26 Aug — businessinsider.com HARD-REJECTS in WebSearch's allowed_domains with a 400, exactly like ft.com and wsj.com, so NEVER put it in a filtered query (it still appears fine in unfiltered results). PREVIOUSLY CONFIRMED 25 Aug — warren.senate.gov (so US SENATE primaries are blocked too; together with the earlier pa.gov result, treat ALL government primaries as unreachable unless proven otherwise, and never spend a call testing one mid-issue) and community.openai.com (OpenAI's developer forum, where its pricing announcements are posted — so a subdomain is NOT a way round the openai.com block). PREVIOUSLY CONFIRMED 23 Aug — axios.com (IMPORTANT: we had been relaying Axios for weeks; it is fetch-blocked, snippet-only), blog.cloudflare.com, developers.cloudflare.com, www.transparencycoalition.ai. So Cloudflare primaries are NOT reachable. PREVIOUSLY CONFIRMED 22 Aug — www-cdn.anthropic.com (so Anthropic's own report PDFs are NOT a way round the anthropic.com block), www.adaptyvbio.com, endpoints.news. Add to the aggregator reject list, from the desk 22 Aug: releasebot.io, aiweekly.co, techstartups.com, valueaddvc.com, explainx.ai, promptailearning.com, note.com/hirokimiyano, aifundingtracker.com, openhosst.com, kie.ai, atoms.dev, evolink.ai, datanorth.ai. PREVIOUSLY — www.pa.gov is egress-blocked (tried and confirmed 19 Aug), so US state-government primaries are not safe to assume reachable; huggingface.co blog pages also unreachable. cnbc.com is WebFetch-blocked even though it works fine as a SEARCH RESULT source, and WebSearch's allowed_domains filter hard-rejects ft.com and wsj.com with a 400, so never put those two in a filtered query. Direct WebFetch otherwise fails on essentially everything — anthropic.com, docs.anthropic.com, openai.com, techcrunch.com, 9to5mac.com, tomshardware.com, datacenterdynamics.com, huggingface.co, aisi.gov.uk, theverge/reuters/arstechnica/zdnet. Assume snippet-only and budget for it; never spend agent calls proving a host is blocked. ONE CONFIRMED EXCEPTION, found Aug 12: platform.claude.com IS REACHABLE and returns full page text — the model-deprecations page is a genuine primary, use it for every obit and any Claude lifecycle claim. Also note: WebSearch's own result summaries frequently quote substantive content from blocked hosts (it summarised anthropic.com's incident post on Aug 12), so a search can sometimes retrieve what a fetch cannot.

- Issue 116 (Sep 3, Thu, HEAVY — four news stories + The Quiet Correction) — 2 charts (confessed-axis slope + the-only-thing-above-the-axis timeline). (1) **MY OWN SWEEP FOUND THREE OF THE FOUR (Astra shipping, the DOJ filing, Meta's undated open weights) AND THE AGENT FOUND THE ONE I HAD NO SIGHT OF AND IT WAS THE BIGGEST — Nvidia CONFIRMING Hugging Face.** That is the second time in three issues the agent's single unique find was the story I would have missed entirely; the case for running both sweeps is settled and should stop being re-argued. (2) **THE AGENT LED WITH TWO STORIES WE PUBLISHED YESTERDAY — Anthropic's EFS and Astra's Critical classification — for the SEVENTH consecutive run.** Issue 100's check-the-lead-against-back-issues rule remains the single most valuable line in this file. LEFT col = capability and who is allowed to have it (Astra's autonomy, Meta's undated weights); RIGHT = ownership and the record (the commons sold, the training data litigated, the roadmap that quietly wasn't). Corrections satire = this publication is available to a limited set of organisations and to everyone else in the coming days, and it has been the coming days for some time. Footer kicker = not yet cleared for the AGI era. ## 112 CHART INSTRUMENTS (archived 6 Sep 2026 from memory.md)
- 112's TIMELINE is the first in this paper to draw a RUST SEGMENT rather than rust TICKS: the fourteen days between the merger becoming effective (14 Aug) and OpenAI's notice (28–29 Aug) are thickened into a rust bar ON the axis with a measured bracket and "14 days" above it, while every earlier event is a plain gray tick and the future endpoint (12 Nov) is a HOLLOW navy dot because OpenAI calls it proposed. The argument is that the INTERVAL, not any event, is the news. New after 110's arrowhead, 107's question-mark tick and 106's open caret. Reusable whenever the story is the GAP BETWEEN two events rather than either of them — and the hollow-endpoint-for-a-proposed-date trick is separable and reusable on its own.
- 112's BAR is a DRAWN-FROM-ZERO BAR and the honesty of the baseline IS the argument: three near-identical bars (Hy4 2.99, Kimi K3 2.94, GLM-5.3 2.92, out of 4), winner in rust and the rest gray, so the claimed victory visibly vanishes; the axis label row reads "163 experts · 203 engineering tasks · scored out of 4 · run by the winner" and the caption states the gap is 0.07 of 4, under two per cent. New bar variant after 110's category-error bar, 108's sub-pixel subject and 105's uncertainty tail — and distinct from 108, which made a REAL number vanish for being small, where this makes a CLAIMED GAP vanish for being narrow. Reusable on any vendor-run eval reporting a thin margin: draw it from zero and let the tie show. BUMP still unused since 43 — needs rank-over-time for SEVERAL entities; keep waiting, do not force it. Standing move from 108, still unused: a chart REFUSED IN THE PROSE ("there is no chart on this item because there is nothing to put beside it") beats a silent omission — reusable when a number has no honest comparator, and 111 had three chartless stories that could each have carried it.

## NVIDIA STRUCTURED NON-ACQ (archived 6 Sep 2026 from memory.md)
- NVIDIA'S STRUCTURED NON-ACQUISITIONS (issue 107 LEAD, NEW — now a standing beat) — three deals, same three parts each: a licence that is NOT an acquisition, job offers rather than a staff transfer, and the target left standing as a formal competitor. (1) ENFABRICA, Sept 2025, >$900m cash+stock, licensed networking/memory tech, hired CEO Rochan Sankar and team, company remains standalone (CNBC 18 Sep 2025). (2) GROQ, Dec 2025, ~$20bn, NON-EXCLUSIVE licence to LPU/inference tech, took founder Jonathan Ross, president Sunny Madra and key engineers, Groq continues independently; Huang's email to staff: "While we are adding talented employees to our ranks and licensing Groq's IP, we are not acquiring Groq as a company"; a CNBC analyst called it keeping "the fiction of competition alive." (3) POOLSIDE, Aug 2026, $6bn NON-EXCLUSIVE licence to Model Factory + $1bn equity at $12bn pre-money + offers to 109 engineers who built Laguna (going to Nemotron); founders Eiso Kant, Jason Warner, Margarida Garcia do NOT go. Poolside context: raised $626m at $3bn late 2024 from Bain Capital Ventures, eBay AND NVIDIA; had a 6-week window to raise $2bn for a 40,000 GB300 cluster due online in January, missed it, lost the cluster. Broke via NEWCOMER 20 Aug, credited by Bloomberg; Forbes/Qz/TNW/PYMNTS 24 Aug. WARREN + BLUMENTHAL wrote to Huang MARCH 2026 asking whether the Groq deal was "structured to evade scrutiny by antitrust regulators" — no answer found, warren.senate.gov is EGRESS-BLOCKED. WATCH: any FTC/DOJ action or second-request, whether Nvidia ever files anything, a FOURTH deal in this form, whether Poolside ever licenses Model Factory to anyone else, and whether any senator follows up.
