## CORRECTIONS CANDIDATES
- Issue 129: **THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG is the LEAD's spine — that the three exclusions in the METR/Redwood scope are the load-bearing fact and that "independence was never the scarce commodity, jurisdiction was."** We never read metr.org or blog.redwoodresearch.org (both blocked) and **the scoping agreement was never published by anybody**, so the six days, the seven questions and the three exclusions all reach us through the investigators' own summary AS RELAYED. If the exclusions were the investigators' own choice rather than OpenAI's, or if a separate workstream covered the safeguards, the headline, the pull quote and the entire chart are a misreading of a sensible research decision — **and we conceded the competent-investigations case in the opinion para and again in the note, which is the only thing keeping it reportage.** SECOND: **the seventh in-scope question is named nowhere we could open**, and we drew it as an unnamed dashed row rather than inventing it; if it turns out to be about the company, the chart's 7/3 split is wrong in our favour. THIRD: **Lehane's no-waiver position is CHARACTERISED, not quoted** — the aviation line we have from two outlets, the waiver line from paraphrase, and no OpenAI primary was read. FOURTH: **the aviation counter-argument is ours** — we assert that the FAA/Congress convened and protected those programmes, and we did not cite a statute or check whether any airline safety-pooling arrangement is in fact purely voluntary. FIFTH: the FRONTIER Act's text was NOT read (congress.gov blocked); the $5bn/$10bn threshold, the Commerce licensing and the developer-picks-its-own-IVO structure are all as secondaries describe them. SIXTH: SoftBank's repayment is an ANNOUNCEMENT dated 9 Sep of an intention for the 15th — **we have still never found a post-facto settlement confirmation and said so on both days.** SEVENTH: the Trump–Xi dialogue is "reportedly centres on" — nothing is convened and we drew no chart, deliberately. **Only first-hand source read this issue: platform.claude.com's deprecations page, which carried nothing usable for the sixteenth consecutive time.**
- Issue 128: **THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG is the LEAD's spine — that China Daily's cooperation paragraph is, in substance, Amodei's step three.** That is our reading of two sentences reaching us through relays, and the whole headline, the pull quote and the note's opener rest on it. If the "planned bilateral AI-safety dialogue" is a boilerplate formula that has appeared in Chinese editorials for a year, or if the cooperation lines sit in a different editorial from the "club" line, the piece is a misreading dressed as close reading. SECOND: **not one Chinese source was read first-hand** — chinadaily.com.cn is blocked, and the Guo Jiakun, Global Times and China Daily quotations all arrive through outlets that relayed them; the China Daily editorial is dated 14 Sep by one outlet and 15 Sep by another and we did not resolve it. THIRD: **"every headline used a verb of attack and the cooperative paragraph was in none of them" is an observation about the ~nine accounts this desk could reach, not about the coverage** — and we phrased it that way, but it is still the weakest empirical claim in the issue. FOURTH: **the essay's China clauses are as secondaries describe them for the fourth day running** — the "militarily dominate democracies" and "grave danger" quotations have never been checked against darioamodei.com, which stays blocked. FIFTH: Coxon's withdrawal is AXIOS-ALONE and no reason was given by anyone. SIXTH: the assembly's ~300 attendance and the speaker list are as NPR/Axios/NewsNation reported; we did not attend and no organiser list was read. SEVENTH: the markets short's index closes are the agent's, from snippets, and nobody printed a Tuesday figure for the three cyber names the whole piece turns on. **Only first-hand source read this issue: platform.claude.com's deprecations page, which carried nothing usable for the fifteenth consecutive time.**
- Issue 127: **THE ONE WE MOST LIKELY GOT WRONG is the LEAD's spine — that Monday's sign split is evidence the market priced Amodei's diagnosis and refused his remedy.** Prices are overdetermined; a ten-year above 5%, crude above $100 and SoftBank's Tuesday repayment were all in the same session, and the cyber bid may be a Fal.Con/analyst-upgrade story that would have happened without an essay. We conceded all of that in the opinion para and in the axis note and still built the headline, the pull quote and the chart on the sign. **If the cyber names give it back inside a week, the whole reading was a one-day artefact.** SECOND: **not one figure in the lead was read off an exchange feed** — every percentage is as a named outlet reported it at an unstated hour, and SoftBank's runs from "more than 10" to 13.2 across outlets. THIRD: **the standards-body second lead is The Information's reporting reaching us through relays only** — theinformation.com AND cnn.com are both blocked, no company has confirmed the meetings, and "since at least July" is the relays' phrase, so if the meetings were occasional rather than a standing working group, "nine weeks old" overstates it and the self-correction against issue 126 is itself too strong. FOURTH: **the July dates are "July"** and we said so on the chart. FIFTH: Sacks's title is unresolved between two outlets, and his post reaches us as quoted fragments, not as a read post. SIXTH: Gomez's quotations come from Globe and Mail plus secondaries of his own blog, which we did not open. **Only first-hand source read this issue: platform.claude.com's deprecations page, which carried nothing usable for the fourteenth consecutive time.**

- EGRESS (standing, halved 13 Sep; history in memory-archive.md): **16 SEP — ONE NEW BLOCKED PRIMARY: `blog.redwoodresearch.org` (the investigation the whole lead turns on; metr.org was already blocked, so BOTH halves of that primary were unreachable). congress.gov confirmed blocked as a government primary, so the FRONTIER Act text went unread. platform.claude.com reachable for the TWENTIETH consecutive run and read in full. 15 SEP — ONE NEW BLOCKED PRIMARY: `chinadaily.com.cn` (the editorial the whole lead turns on). platform.claude.com reachable for the NINETEENTH consecutive run and read in full. 14 SEP — TWO BLOCKED HOSTS: `cnn.com` (the standards-body relay) and `cnbc.com` on FETCH, confirming the search-only rule for it. platform.claude.com reachable for the EIGHTEENTH consecutive run and read in full. 13 SEP — TWO BLOCKED PRIMARIES: `darioamodei.com` (the essay itself) and `sakana.ai` (the Fugu release). Also newly blocked: nbcnews.com. platform.claude.com reachable for the SEVENTEENTH consecutive run and read in full.** **THE RULES, all still in force: assume EVERY primary is unfetchable and treat WebSearch summaries as the NORMAL evidence base, cross-checking a quotation across two or three NAMED outlets and saying in the box you did not read the document · `github.com` IS fetchable, so look for the ARTEFACT not the announcement when the subject is code · when a statement lives on X, SEARCH FOR THE POST TEXT rather than fetching — this worked twice on 13 Sep (Dario's and ClaudeDevs') · NEVER put ft.com, wsj.com, businessinsider.com or theguardian.com in allowed_domains (hard 400, kills the call) · never spend calls proving a host is blocked · THE AGENT'S NEGATIVES ARE NOT EVIDENCE · spend ONE search on a surprising story's DATE and ONE before discarding an aggregator claim · A COLD THREAD IS NOT A DEAD SUBJECT.** Confirmed blocked: chinadaily.com.cn, anthropic.com + all subdomains, openai.com, blog.google, deepmind.google, metr.org, darioamodei.com, sakana.ai, axios.com, theregister.com, engadget.com, thehackernews.com, simonwillison.net, huggingface.co, quantamagazine.org, techcrunch.com, nextgov.com, asml.com, mistral.ai, greynoise.io, venturebeat.com, the-decoder.com, terrytao.wordpress.com, mathandai.org, officechai.com, nbcnews.com, cnn.com, theinformation.com, bloomberg.com, all government primaries, plus cnbc/fortune/qz/washingtonpost on FETCH though fine as search sources. Aggregator reject list: releasebot.io, aiweekly.co, techstartups.com, explainx.ai, cubbbix, techdg, llm-stats, tech-insider.org, kucoin and kin.
- SANDBOX HAZARD (standing, not issue-specific): search results are heavily polluted by AI-generated aggregators (cubbbix, techdg, blog.mean.ceo, buildfastwithai, llm-stats) that fabricate dates, model names and figures. Confirmed fabrications to never print: "OpenAI Astra solved ten open math problems", "UK AI Regulation and Safety Bill cleared the Commons Aug 14", "China fined 12 companion-AI companies 4.2M RMB", "Anthropic $65B Series H at $965B post". Require a named real outlet or a company primary. Also NEVER PRINT: ByteDance "10T params" / "8T Mythos 5" (rumour, seen repeatedly).

## EDITOR'S NOTE PATTERNS
(Do-not-reuse-verbatim ledger: opener → spine → close, one line each. The worn connector to AVOID always: "different companies, same motion." Openers used are also do-not-repeat.)

A genuine CONVERGENCE, so no connector joins the paragraphs and none appears. Distinct from 126's person-or-corporation, which sorted by WHO AUTHORED; from 128's holds-the-lever, which sorted by WHETHER THE SPEAKER CONTROLS THE MECHANISM; from 125's sanction-available; and from 123's who-bears-the-cost) → IN-THE-ESSAY-IN-THE-BILL-AND-IN-THE-ONE-EXERCISE-ANYBODY-HAS-CARRIED-OUT-THE-SUBJECT-PICKS-THE-EXAMINER-AND-WRITES-THE-EXAM (**WHO-HOLDS-THE-PEN close**: name the single structural feature common to all three versions of the proposal, state that not one person that week proposed changing it, and impute no bad faith; new close shape, deliberately NOT 124's only-the-giver-can-withdraw, which was about WHO MAY RETRACT, NOT 127's instrument-arrives-afterwards, which was about SEQUENCE, and NOT 128's empty-chair; issue 129). **THE CREDIT RUNS AT FULL LENGTH INSIDE THE OPENING PARAGRAPH AND IS NEVER TAKEN BACK — "nobody has suggested they were leaned on", "they were right", "no other frontier laboratory has permitted anything close" — and the paragraph turns only in its last sentence. ## EDITOR'S NOTEBOOK
- Issue 129 (Sep 16, Wed — FOUR items: two leads, two shorts, no standing feature) — 2 charts (a bar with no quantity in it + a normalised three-line slope). (1) **THE LEAD CAME FROM READING A THREE-WEEK-OLD DOCUMENT INSTEAD OF THE DAY'S NEWS.** Every outlet ran Wednesday's evaluator story as "will they really be independent?"; the answer had been published on 26 August by the people who did it. **NEW STANDING MOVE: when a policy argument is about whether a safeguard would work, search for whether anybody has already run it — and when you find the pilot, read its SCOPE rather than its findings.** This is the natural successor to 128's read-past-the-verb and it found a better story than the day's actual news. (2) **BOTH SWEEPS LANDED THE SAME LEAD AND BOTH MISSED HALF OF IT.** My own sweep found the Lehane confirmation on a generic date query; the agent found SoftBank, the Fed and the Trump–Xi summit and none of the evaluator story. **The 125 amendment holds: run both, rank neither. The agent's DISCARD PILE again did real work — the Trump–Xi/Altman state-dinner thread came out of it.** (3) **THE FILE CAUGHT THE FLAGGED FABRICATION AGAIN.** The agent handed us "$65bn Series H at $965bn post-money" for the third recorded time. It is barred, it was not printed, and **the reject list alone now justifies reading this file in full every morning.** (4) **A WATCH ITEM DYING IS ALSO A RESULT.** Brazil's Bill 2338/2023 turned out to be a confused item we had been carrying on an agent's say-so — killed, with the reason written down. The Sanders–Casar introduction date is now contradicted between our own 128 (Sanders's "next week") and the agent ("already introduced 4 Sep") — **RESOLVE BEFORE PRINTING EITHER.** (5) REFUSALS: OpenAI's Sponsored Agents launch (real, but the meter-inside-the-answer angle is spent and four strong items beat five); the 404 Media "Project Lily" contractor story (one outlet, and it needed a day); GPT-Live vs Gemini 3.8 Live pricing (my sweep's only source was aggregator-tier); and **A CHART IN THE BEIJING SHORT, REFUSED IN THE PROSE — that clears the chart-refused-in-the-prose device after EIGHT issues on the shelf, and it is now spent rather than retired.** (6) Corrections satire = a paper that sets the scope of its own corrections and retains itself as its own independent verification organisation. Footer kicker = out of scope by mutual agreement. (7) Housekeeping: compressed the 110/111 and 123/124 ANGLES, the 98–101 note shapes and 126's corrections candidates into pointers; closed the 14 Sep selloff thread unanswered; halved the standing-features line. **NEXT RUN: resolve the Sanders–Casar introduction date, and watch the 24 Sep Trump–Xi week — it is the densest dated cluster this paper has ever had on the calendar.**

## ARCHIVED 18 SEP 2026 (issue 131) — issue 130 notebook + corrections candidates, superseded
- **Issue 130 (Sep 17, Thu — FIVE items: two leads, three shorts, no standing feature) — 2 charts (three-state dumbbell + business-day timeline).** (1) **MY OWN SWEEP FOUND BOTH LEADS AND THE AGENT FOUND NEITHER OF THEM AS LEADS** — a generic "AI news September 17 2026" query returned the Anthropic index and the OpenAI framework in its first result block. The agent, well-scoped and honest, led on a product story. **The 125 amendment holds harder than ever: run both sweeps, rank neither, and run the generic date query FIRST.** (2) **THE AGENT'S REAL VALUE WAS THE DATED ITEMS AND THE DISCARD PILE** — it resolved the Sanders–Casar introduction question we have carried for three issues (announced, never introduced, no number), supplied the FRONTIER Act co-sponsor list, confirmed the S-1 negative, and handed us the Hinton briefing and the Cowork merge. Two of the five items are its. **Scope the agent to DATED WATCH ITEMS and let your own sweep hunt leads.** (3) **THE FILE CAUGHT THE FLAGGED FABRICATION FOR THE FOURTH TIME** ($65bn Series H at $965bn post) — the agent flagged it itself this run, which is new and good. (4) **A CORRECTION WE OWED OURSELVES RAN IN THE BODY, NOT THE BOX** — the Sanders–Casar bill was never introduced and we had implied twice that it was. Second issue running that a self-correction sat inside the story that produced it; that is now the house method. (5) REFUSALS: the Fed aftermath (129 owned it and Thursday's US closes were unobtainable); Apple's M8 Ultra server (single-source, 2029, conditional); the OpenAI Sponsored Agents ad rollout (spent angle, refused again); the protest reporting outside the Scottish meeting (one outlet, and it named an attendee no other list had). (6) **MEMORY REWRITTEN TO ONE-LINE ENTRIES** — the file had reached 142KB of 3,000-character "lines", a compounding daily token cost; full prior text appended to memory-archive.md. Keep it lean: if an entry runs past one line next run, cut it. (7) Corrections satire = a paper declining to publish its own automation level. Footer kicker = no measured category reached AL5. **NEXT RUN: watch the 24 Sep Trump–Xi week, and check whether anybody outside Anthropic computes the three metrics.**
- **Issue 130, the one we most likely got wrong: the LEAD's spine — that the index is "unreproducible by construction".** We never read anthropic.com's methodology (blocked); if the published method includes an externally-auditable task taxonomy, or if Epoch itself rated the tasks, the blade is wrong and the credit paragraph is the whole story. SECOND: the "under 1% in January" baseline is Bloomberg/AlphaSignal's characterisation ("effectively nothing at the start of the year"), not a figure we saw stated as under 1% — the dumbbell's left dot is therefore ours. THIRD: whether AL3+ and AL4 are strictly nested is OUR reading of "AL3 or higher"; we said so on the chart. FOURTH: OpenAI's three tracks and their day-counts reach us via CNBC/NBC/Quartz; we did not read the framework, and the "slow track is for third parties" clause — the second lead's whole hinge — is a single relayed characterisation. FIFTH: the six incidents' six-month window is CNBC's "since March"; one outlet gave Oct 2025–Jul 2026 and we did not print it. SIXTH: Tegmark and Cotra at the Sanders briefing come from one headline. **Only first-hand source read: platform.claude.com's deprecations page, nothing usable for the seventeenth consecutive time.**

- **Issue 131, the one we most likely got wrong: we assert the fourth fair-use factor is "the one that decides commercial cases and the one the defendants have to win".** That is our legal characterisation, not a quotation from any filing or ruling, and courts weight the factors less mechanically than that sentence implies. SECOND: every quotation in the lead — Hecht's two documents, Turley's note, the Brockman exchange, the 160,903 figure and the Project Mango description — reaches us through TechCrunch/TheWrap/WaPo relaying a brief we did not open; courtlistener and sec.gov are beyond this desk. THIRD: we say Hecht "was objecting to" his employer rather than confessing for it — that is our reading of a memo we have seen two sentences of. FOURTH: Healey's letter and the S.3228 §164 thresholds are as the Boston Globe, NBC Boston and WWLP report them; we did not read the letter or the section. FIFTH: "an incident that killed forty-nine people would not trigger the reporting duty" is our arithmetic on a relayed summary of the draft, and the draft has other triggers (compromise, loss of control causing injury) that might catch it. SIXTH: the DeepMind Institute's director list and the claim that all three are Google-paid comes from Axios/TechCrunch/TNW snippets. **Only first-hand source read: platform.claude.com's deprecations page, nothing usable for the eighteenth consecutive time.**

[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 132's TIMELINE: a six-day axis (12–18 Sep) with rust ticks only on the two events somebody SIGNED and gray on the arguments around them, the axis continuing as a DASHED arrow past the right edge because the two live deadlines (16 Nov 2026, 1 May 2027) are off-scale, and a rust axis note naming the thing no tick represents — a standard for what an evaluator may publish. **Reusable whenever a story's live deadlines are an order of magnitude further out than its events.**
[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 132's BAR: six quantities all drawn to ONE scale so the actual booked revenue renders as a 1.5px hairline against a $103bn order book. The caption says drawing it honestly is the argument. **Reusable whenever a promise and a realised figure differ by three orders of magnitude — resist the urge to log-scale, the invisibility IS the finding.**
[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 131's PICTOGRAM: 161 squares at 1,000 works each, the 161st drawn at 45% opacity as the remaining 903, and a rust ARROW off the right edge labelled "AT LEAST" because the brief pleads a floor. **Reusable whenever a plaintiff's or a company's own count is a MINIMUM rather than a total — the arrow does the epistemics the number can't.**
[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 131's SLOPE: two stacked bands, each normalised to its OWN left-hand value because the units differ (deaths, dollars), with the liberty confessed on the axis; endpoint sub-labels carry the argument ("FIFTY TIMES EARLIER") so the line carries only the number. Rust axis note names the value NOT drawn — the number the conferees will actually agree. **Reusable for any two-variable before/after where the units refuse to share a scale.**
[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 130's DUMBBELL (superseded in part by 133's, keep for the empty-row move): three rows in three different states — a row with NO LEFT-HAND DOT and a dashed line running the full range to a left-pointing arrowhead ("no baseline published, the line could start anywhere along it"), a row with a real gap, and an ENTIRELY EMPTY ROW carrying only a pale dashed rule and a centred gray label. Axis note confesses that the rows are NESTED not exclusive; rust axis note names the reading nobody outside took. **Reusable whenever a published scale has rungs the subject reported unevenly.**
[chart instrument, archived from memory.md 21 Sep] - 130's TIMELINE: the axis is BUSINESS DAYS FROM AN EVENT rather than calendar dates, two gray ticks at the published deadlines (one above the axis, one below) and a rust segment leaving the chart on an arrowhead for the track with no stated maximum. **Reusable whenever a policy states some deadlines and not others — put the unstated one off the edge.**
